# validator-8: W4H-35 validation plan — W16 Kubernetes profile, resource profiles, wrapped-key material (prepared; extended later)
Brief w00/briefs/w4h-35.md a694ea90849cd2dd. Branch wp/w4h-35-w16-k8s-profiles from 390c49a90. Standing rules:
plan-common-w4h-32-36.md 147bb9ec215b3a14 (S1-S10).

V1 Rows first: C12 steps for the Kubernetes profile; the resource profile shape; the key-material format.
V2 Pins: every image digest-pinned; deployment-locks extended (one lock mechanism; git grep for a second); no Docker Hub pull
   without the team mirror; the pins checked by the existing locks check (mutant: one tag instead of a digest -> red).
V3 Kubernetes profile on kind or k3s in Podman [H]: from clean state, the seven C12 steps in order and idempotent (a second
   run changes nothing; I diff the cluster objects); supervisors as DaemonSets with runtime access and host identity and NO
   tenant secret (I read the pod specs: env, mounts, secrets); health checks; bounded resources; trusted ingress identity.
   A forced migration/readiness failure stops the wave (no later step runs). Teardown: credentials fail afterwards;
   retention holds. Distinct identities for separate installations (two installs here; the ten-install campaign is W19).
V4 Resource profiles: GPU and CPU-only, selected by the installation; the CPU-only profile runs the reference pack end to end
   on this host's proof path with the TEST provider (no model call); a GPU profile without a GPU is refused by name. [H]
   (team-lead's word before a long lock).
V5 Wrapped-key material: generated apart from application secrets; private file format; 0600 files, 0700 dirs, process
   ownership verified by tests; rotation and revocation proven (old key refused after revocation); idempotent rerun; no
   key bytes in any log (sweep).
V6 Operator docs under docs/: prerequisites, locks, secret references, recovery, drain, upgrades, rollback, CPU-only
   availability; each command in the docs is the one the tests run (spot-check by grep).
V7 Isolation: kind/k3s and its images are mine to remove only if I created them; the owner's clusters are not touched.
Verdict per S10. REJECT on: an unpinned image; a tenant secret in a supervisor; a non-idempotent step; key material readable
by group/other.

## Addendum A (2026-10-16, validator-8): owner report 1 (inventory, rows 1-7) and team-lead's rulings
Existing: kubernetes-profile.ts is a renderer only (no FactoryKubernetesTarget; nothing applies, waits or purges); four open
w16 gate rows (hosted-supervisor-readiness, hosted-supervisor-address, hosted-ingress-snippet, hosted-host-identity-shared);
the kind proof was admission-only with kindest/node BY TAG; the locks check covers Compose only; a GPU profile is not
declarable by the fleet (host.ts:383 gpuHosts: []); key material exists (master.key 32 raw bytes, wraps.json, private-files
checks, a KMS seam) but no master-key rotation or revocation at the provisioner. Rulings: image pulls by digest allowed (stop
on HTTP 429); tools from nix-shell with their store paths recorded; heavy legs through gated-flock 60-90 min accepted;
hosted-host-identity-shared stays an open row; the resource profile is per FLEET; the two stale statements are fixed in the
package.
Checks by row (rows before code, S2):
- R1 Kubernetes step-5 target: the same seven C12 steps as Compose (one ordered list in code; git grep for a second);
  Secrets created from the delivery dirs BY DATA: the Secret values never appear in a rendered manifest file, a log, or a
  kubectl output in the evidence (sweep with the fixture secret values as markers); a rerun changes nothing (I diff the
  cluster objects' generation and resourceVersion before/after).
- R2 network readiness source + cluster Service names close hosted-supervisor-readiness and hosted-supervisor-address: each
  gate row turned green by a test that would fail without the change (red first).
- R3 trusted ingress = the same pinned nginx renderer as Compose (one renderer; the Kubernetes exception removed; a test
  renders both and compares the trust config); hosted-ingress-snippet closed by a test.
- R4 locks cover Kubernetes images; kindest/node and every in-cluster fixture BY DIGEST; the renderer refuses an unpinned
  image by name; mutants: a tag instead of a digest -> refused; the locks check skips the Kubernetes set -> red.
- R5 resource profile shape at fleet level: GPU and CPU-only declarable; a GPU profile on a host without a GPU refused by
  name; the CPU-only proof runs the reference pack end to end with the TEST provider (no model call) [H, team-lead's word].
- R6 key-material life: rotate creates v<n+1>, keeps prior wraps readable; revoke only when every wrap is under the current
  key (else refused by name, nothing changed); both idempotent (a rerun is a no-op); files 0600, dirs 0700, owner = the
  process user (tests); no key bytes in any log or evidence (sweep). Mutants: revoke with an old wrap left -> red; rotate
  that drops prior wraps -> red.
- R7 local proof (kind in Podman, labelled local): proof A clean install through all seven steps; proof B a forced canary
  failure stops the wave (no later step runs; the record names the step); supervisors as DaemonSets with runtime access and
  host identity and no tenant secret (I read the pod specs in the evidence). [H]
- Tools: kind/kubectl/k3s from nix-shell; the store paths and versions recorded in the report; I check the recorded paths
  exist and match the versions.
- The two stale statements fixed in the package: named in the report with file:line; I read both.
- Isolation: the owner's cluster and images are removed by name after the proof (listing checked); I create my own only if
  I re-run a proof, and remove only mine.

## Addendum B (2026-10-16, validator-8): owner report 2 (rows at 621d03cb1, G2-G4, Q6 ruled (a))
Facts: rows committed at 621d03cb1 (row 5 fleet-level: under gpu the fleet host is the pool's one GPU host; gpu -> cpu-only
needs a new fleet). Tools: kind 0.32.0, kubectl 1.37.1, kubeconform 0.8.0 via nix-shell (store paths recorded). Not yet run
by the owner: typecheck, the PostgreSQL suite, coverage, boundaries, gate-integrity. Q6 ruled (a): only the owner's kind node
joins the existing Podman networks (connect/disconnect by name); shared containers are never modified; its databases and
buckets carry a prefix and are dropped at cleanup; credentials env-only; rows 2 and 7 amended with a service-side address
block.
Checks added:
- B1 order: 621d03cb1 (rows, incl. the row 2/7 amendments) precedes each code commit (git log).
- B2 G2 fleet resources {cpu-only | gpu}: a gpu-host runner profile under cpu-only is refused at the settings read, by name;
  the profile file is validated, 0600, and delivered to the pool only (not to supervisors or other roles: I list the
  delivery targets); installations.cpuCapacity removed: schema drift clean, a migration if a column existed, and no reader
  left (git grep cpuCapacity); the two stale statements read at their file:line. Mutant: the cpu-only refusal dropped -> red.
- B3 G3 master key: rotate creates v<n+1> and keeps prior wraps readable; the wrap file is the one source (git grep for a
  second); a rotation interrupted after the write is resumable (a test that stops between steps and reruns: same final
  state, no second key); revoke is idempotent and only for OLDER versions (revoking the current key refused by name); a wrap
  under a revoked key -> factory_key_missing; 0600 files, 0700 dirs, owner uid tests; the fleet CLI exits non-zero on every
  refusal (the W4H-27 D2 lesson: a CLI must not exit 0 on failure). Mutants: revoke the current key allowed -> red; rotate
  drops a prior wrap -> red; CLI exit 0 on a refusal -> red.
- B4 G4 locks: check-factory-deployment-locks scans deploy/factory/kubernetes/*.yml; kind-proof.yml pins kindest/node
  v1.36.1 by digest; I list all 14 image references and check each is "@sha256:" with 64 hex; the renderer refuses an unpinned
  image (deployment_image_unpinned). Mutants: a tag in one Kubernetes file -> locks check red; the renderer check removed ->
  red.
- B5 Q6 network join (heavy proof): before and after the proof I record (read only) the shared containers' podman inspect
  (networks, mounts, env names) and the shared networks' member lists; the shared containers' config must be byte-identical;
  the kind node is connected and later disconnected BY NAME (its own container name in the log); no other container is
  connected or disconnected.
- B6 prefixed resources: every database and bucket the proof creates carries the owner's prefix; at cleanup each is dropped
  by name; a before/after listing (names only) of the shared PostgreSQL databases and the shared buckets shows the same set
  except the prefixed ones, which are gone.
- B7 credentials env-only: no credential in argv (process listing during the proof), in a rendered manifest, a kubectl
  output or a log (sweep with marker values); the service-side address block in rows 2 and 7 matches the rendered config.
- B8 the not-yet-run legs (typecheck, PostgreSQL suite, coverage, boundaries, gate-integrity) are required before the freeze.

## Addendum C (2026-10-16, validator-8): owner report 3 (rows 2 and 3 built) and Q7 ruled (a)
Facts (uncommitted beyond the staged G2+G3): row 2: the pool and the supervisor serve their latest readiness record on
authenticated routes (GET /v1/pool/readiness; GET /v1/host/readiness for admitted peers only); verifiers split from file
readers so a network record is judged exactly as a file; startup field hostReadiness "network" (one source only, refused by
name otherwise). Row 3: no annotation Ingress; the hosted ingress is the Compose platform's pinned nginx with the same
rendered files in the system namespace, NodePort, rolling update maxUnavailable 0; the harness keeps the proof check in both
profiles; a Kubernetes deployment target (apply/ready/remove/stop/start/purge) with one kubectl helper (Secrets on stdin
only; server-side apply); ~400 tests pass. Q7 ruled (a): a static podman-remote client pinned by sha256 in the Dockerfile and
version-matched to Podman 5.x, recorded in deployment-locks; the node's Podman API socket is a named operator prerequisite
(C12 row + docs); the runner probe fails by name without the socket; for the local proof only its kind node mounts this host's
rootless socket; W4H-27's shipped-isolation check and the image build legs rerun; the hosted "Factory image under shipped
isolation" job must stay green.
Checks added:
- C1 row 2 routes: GET /v1/pool/readiness and /v1/host/readiness refuse an unauthenticated request and a non-admitted peer
  (by name); the network record and the file record of the same state are judged identically (one verifier; a test feeds
  both); hostReadiness accepts exactly one source and refuses two or an unknown one by name; mutant: the route without the
  peer check -> red.
- C2 row 3 ingress: one nginx renderer for both profiles (a test compares the rendered files byte for byte); no annotation
  Ingress object in any Kubernetes file (grep); rolling update maxUnavailable 0; the proof check present in both profiles.
- C3 deployment target and the kubectl helper: one helper (git grep for a second kubectl call path); Secrets passed on stdin
  only (never argv: a test spies the spawn arguments; the evidence's process listing); server-side apply; apply/ready/
  remove/stop/start/purge each by a test; purge removes only the installation's own objects (label selector checked).
- C4 Q7 podman-remote: the Dockerfile pins the artifact by sha256 (64 hex) and the build verifies it (a wrong sha fails the
  build: mutant); the client version matches the Podman 5.x server (the version string recorded; client major = server
  major); deployment-locks records it (locks check red if the pin is removed); the image diff against the previous build is
  ONLY the client binary (I compare the two images' file lists: one added file, nothing else changed; layer sizes recorded).
- C5 probe: without the Podman API socket the runner probe fails by name (a test and one real run); with the socket the
  probe passes; the socket is a named prerequisite in the C12 row and the docs.
- C6 local proof: only the kind node mounts this host's rootless socket (pod spec read: the mount on the supervisor DaemonSet
  only; no other pod mounts it).
- C7 reruns: W4H-27's shipped-isolation check (scripts/check-factory-image-shipped-config.ts) and the image build legs in the
  lock [H]; the hosted "Factory image under shipped isolation" job green on the first hosted run (I read that job's log).

G10-4 as written in Addendum I ("0 model operations") is REPLACED by this; Addendum I stays as it was. Reason: the graph
proof's own assertion requires node B to have exactly one model operation, served by the test provider in mode mock.
G10-4 (binding): the run record <label>.json (label w4h35-cpu-only), read raw:
- outcome "passed";
- installationModelProvider and its pin = the test provider (mock mode);
- node B: exactly 1 completed model operation, by the test provider; every other node: 0;
- a new record field resourceProfile = { profile: "cpu-only", cpuCapacity: 4 }, written by C5 from the same constant the
  stack passes to factoryPoolResources (one source: git grep; a mutant of the constant changes the record or turns a test red);

== 2026-10-17T05:24:47Z F-b read-only root cause (my read; the owner must confirm E2E, red first): the DRIVER, repro/kind-proof.sh 06ee6c3e5a90ca0c line 46 subnet() = podman network inspect --format "{{range .Subnets}}{{.Subnet}}{{end}}" joins the dual-stack kind network subnets (IPv6 /64 + IPv4 /24) with no separator; line 148 puts that one string in nodeCidrs. The product (src/factory/provisioning fleet.ts:71/132, fleet-profile.ts:74, kubernetes-profile.ts:204/212 at 0df478ce3) passes nodeCidrs through unchanged; it does NOT validate a CIDR. So: (1) the driver fix: one array element per subnet (both families, or a ruled family), red first; (2) a product gap, my recommendation: the fleet spec refuses a malformed CIDR by name (nodeCidrs and egressCidrs) before any apply, red test + contract row. Without (2), a bad CIDR reaches the cluster API and fails late or not at all.

== 2026-10-17T05:26:25Z CORRECTION to my F-b read above (W4H-35 owner facts, verified by me): the product DOES check nodeCidrs and egressCidrs: fleet.ts:120 CIDR = /^(?:\d{1,3}\.){3}\d{1,3}\/\d{1,2}$/, :134 nodeCidrs.every(CIDR.test), :139 egressCidrs the same, at 0df478ce3. My search missed line 134 because my filter grep -v test dropped every line that contains "CIDR.test(". Run 5 failed at that product check: hosted-settings.ts:9 -> parseFactoryFleetSettings (fleet.ts:192) -> "fleet_settings_invalid: Fleet settings are invalid: kubernetes." (logs/kind-proof-launch.nohup lines 17-31), after image-import, before any apply. So the product refused the malformed CIDR, as it must. The only product gap is the message: it names the group "kubernetes", not the field "kubernetes.nodeCidrs". F-a: fleet-init.log is the informational result JSON {"settings":...,"releaseRefused":[...]}, not the failure. The driver fix stays required (subnet() one CIDR per line; IPv4 subnets for nodeCidrs/egressCidrs, as the product contract is IPv4; a dual-stack stub test, red first). My earlier "the product does not validate" was WRONG; I withdraw it.

== 2026-10-17T05:28:06Z team-lead rulings recorded (driver fix approved; field-named refusal IN SCOPE before the freeze, not a run-6 gate; the IPv6/dual-stack doc line plus a leftover; teardown guard; the kind network removed by name after 2 checks plus a marker; run 6 on my OK by hash of the driver fix, guard and marker). My two settled items:
- Item 2, E2E path trace at 0df478ce3, with test files excluded by PATH (my lesson): the only production path to a NetworkPolicy ipBlock is fleet-cli.ts:236 loadFactoryFleetSettings -> fleet.ts:232 parseFactoryFleetSettings (the CIDR check at :134/:139) -> fleet-cli.ts:237 composeFactoryProvisioner -> fleet.ts:333 factoryFleetProfile -> fleet-profile.ts:152 kubernetesProfile -> :126 new FactoryKubernetesTarget (the ONLY non-test construction) -> kubernetes-target.ts:116/200/229 renderFactoryKubernetes* -> kubernetes-profile.ts:204/212. fleet-cli.ts:300 (the other parse call) builds a compose profile. So NO path reaches the ipBlock without the parser: no product validation change. (The render functions are exported, so a future caller could skip the parser; that is a note, not a finding.)
- Item 5, F-a confirmed: scripts/factory-fleet-init-local.ts:60 refuses placeholder-digest adapters by name; scripts/factory-fleet-init-local.test.ts:29 asserts that exact message; the driver runs with set -euo pipefail, and run 5 went past fleet-init (its result JSON was written) to fail at hosted-settings.ts:9, so fleet-init exited 0. No install, no preinstall, no fleet; no G8 line (correct: the evidence for it does not exist).
Post-run checks PASS: the shared stores UNCHANGED vs c2033a21765d18d6 (field compare now); the run folder has 0 PEM or kubeconfig keys and 0 quoted key literals; stores-modes.txt 700/755/644/644; cleanup "down ok; left 0" and "done; left: containers=0 volumes=0 networks=0 credentials=0 scratch=0"; the kind network removed and logged; my check: 0 w4h35 containers, networks (and no kind), volumes, store folders, scratch roots; ~/.w4h35-fleet and ~/.w4h35-runner absent.
ROOT CAUSE (my read; the owner confirms E2E): for an image imported by "ctr images import" from an archive, crictl lists a DIGEST column (693171bda9d16...) but records NO repoDigests (there is no registry), so node_image_ref (which requires exactly one repoDigest) finds none. Also: "ctr import --digests" names the digest record "import-2026-10-17@sha256:693171bd...", not "localhost/w4h35-factory@sha256:...", so a pod reference repo@digest may still not resolve even with the right digest. The nodeimage stub test passed only because its stub returned a repoDigest that real crictl does not give. Required: build the stub from real crictl/ctr output of an archive-imported image; a fix that gives the node a record named exactly by the pinned repo@digest (for example a ctr images tag of the imported image to localhost/w4h35-factory@sha256:<target digest>) and proves that crictl inspecti of that exact reference resolves, red first; then the pre-install check proves it again before the install.
